## Introduction
Below is a diagram of the transformer architecture as it appeared in the paper [Attention Is All You Need](https://arxiv.org/abs/1706.03762). The diagram presents the encoder-decoder architecture and tracks the shape of tensors as they are passed through the network. Hover over parts of the diagram to learn more about their transformation.

  &lt;mxCell id=\&quot;0\&quot; /&gt;\n        &lt;mxCell id=\&quot;1\&quot; parent=\&quot;0\&quot; /&gt;\n        &lt;UserObject label=\&quot;\&quot; tooltip=\&quot;&amp;lt;pre&amp;gt;&amp;#xa;import numpy as np&amp;#xa;import matplotlib.pyplot as plt&amp;#xa;&amp;#xa;def sample_dot_products(M, max_dim, scaling = False):&amp;#xa;    dims = np.arange(1, max_dim + 1)&amp;#xa;    avg_abs_dot_products = []&amp;#xa;    &amp;#xa;    for d in dims:&amp;#xa;        X = np.random.multivariate_normal(mean=np.zeros(d), cov=np.identity(d), size = M)&amp;#xa;        Y = np.random.multivariate_normal(mean=np.zeros(d), cov=np.identity(d), size = M)&amp;#xa;&amp;#xa;        if scaling:&amp;#xa;            factor = 1 / np.sqrt(d)&amp;#xa;        else:&amp;#xa;            factor = 1&amp;#xa;&amp;#xa;        dots = (X @ Y.T) * factor&amp;#xa;        &amp;#xa;        avg_abs_dot_products.append(np.mean(np.abs(dots)))&amp;#xa;    &amp;#xa;    return dims, avg_abs_dot_products&amp;#xa;&amp;#xa;M = 1000  # Number of dot products sampled per dimension&amp;#xa;max_dim = 100  # Maximum dimension&amp;#xa;&amp;#xa;dims, avg_abs_dot_products = sample_dot_products(M, max_dim)&amp;#xa;dims, scaled_avg_abs_dot_products = sample_dot_products(M, max_dim, scaling=True)&amp;#xa;&amp;#xa;&amp;#xa;fig, ax = plt.subplots(1, 2, figsize=(10, 5))&amp;#xa;&amp;#xa;ax[0].scatter(dims, avg_abs_dot_products, marker=&amp;#39;.&amp;#39;, linestyle=&amp;#39;-&amp;#39;)&amp;#xa;ax[0].set_xlabel(&amp;quot;Dimension&amp;quot;)&amp;#xa;ax[0].set_ylabel(&amp;quot;Mean Dot Product Magnitude&amp;quot;)&amp;#xa;ax[0].set_title(&amp;quot;Unscaled Dot Product Magnitude vs. Dimension&amp;quot;)&amp;#xa;ax[0].grid()&amp;#xa;&amp;#xa;ax[1].scatter(dims, scaled_avg_abs_dot_products, marker=&amp;#39;.&amp;#39;, linestyle=&amp;#39;-&amp;#39;)&amp;#xa;ax[1].set_xlabel(&amp;quot;Dimension&amp;quot;)&amp;#xa;ax[1].set_ylabel(&amp;quot;Mean Dot Product Magnitude&amp;quot;)&amp;#xa;ax[1].set_title(&amp;quot;Square Root Scaled Dot Product Magnitude vs. Dimension&amp;quot;)&amp;#xa;ax[1].grid()&amp;#xa;&amp;#xa;plt.tight_layout()&amp;#xa;&amp;lt;/pre&amp;gt;\&quot; id=\&quot;V5DlkOZeu79TRMl4xZtO-1\&quot;&gt;\n          &lt;mxCell id=\&quot;X5mycXT-UQ77Il74ekUl-96\&quot; value=\&quot;&amp;lt;div&amp;gt;Given a sequence of \\(T_d\\) tokens, the decoder has access to tokens 1 through \\(T_d\\) during its forward pass. Consider one of these tokens, token \\(j\\), where \\(1 \\leq j \\leq T_d\\).&amp;lt;/div&amp;gt;&amp;lt;div&amp;gt;&amp;lt;br&amp;gt;&amp;lt;/div&amp;gt;&amp;lt;div&amp;gt;During one forward pass in training, the decoder takes a look at each token \\(j\\) and makes a guess for the \\(j+1\\)-th token. This is done for all \\(j\\) in a single forward pass. Since token \\(j+1\\) is present in the sequence already, we need to hide it from the decoder and make the decoder guess using only the information up to and including token \\(j\\).&amp;lt;/div&amp;gt;&amp;lt;div&amp;gt;&amp;lt;br&amp;gt;&amp;lt;/div&amp;gt;So, token \\(j\\) should &amp;quot;see&amp;quot; only itself and the tokens that preceded it. So, the query for token \\(j\\) should only attend to the key for token \\(j\\) and the keys of tokens preceding token \\(j\\).
